#### **SOFTWARE-DEFINED RADIOS** 

I-29. Software-defined radios function as a family of advanced multiband, multimode, net-centric-capable radios. The software-defined radio’s operating system enables the interaction between the radio hardware components and waveform application software. With legacy radios, the hardware determined the function and operation of the radio. For software-defined radios, the hardware and software are less rigidly coupled. This provides greater interoperability with waveform applications and network management tools while minimizing interoperability issues associated with upgrades to the radio’s operating system. Current software-defined radios include program of record systems and commercial acquisition solutions. 

**164** 

**ATP 6-02.53** 

**31 July 2025** 

**Tactical Radio Platforms** 

##### **RADIO WAVEFORMS** 

I-30. Software-defined radio waveforms serve as software applications that provide the capabilities needed to operate over-the-air communications, including interoperability with most legacy military radios. Waveforms include network configurations, frequency bands, electromagnetic counter-countermeasures and operating modes, channel presets, voice and data processing, and signal processing. Waveforms designs support all domains including air, land, and maritime platforms, command posts, Soldier portable and carried systems, and software-defined radios that directly support special applications and legacy functions such as SINCGARS. Examples of current advanced waveforms include— 

- TSM. 

- WREN-TSM. 

- WREN-Narrowband. 

- MUOS. 

###### **Tactical Scalable Mobile Ad Hoc Network** 

I-31. TSM is a commercial development that supports networked voice, data and position location information capabilities. TSM provides a resilient, self-healing, wideband mesh configuration to support talk groups over a single-channel. TSM variants include an enhanced mode with greater throughput, flexible bandwidth, network adaptability, and waveform portability. TSMx, is a vendor assigned designator, which is specifically designed to support  interface with National Security Agency-certified type-1 security architectures up to and including secret level classification. 

###### **Warrior Robust Enhanced Network Tactical Scalable Mobile Ad Hoc Network** 

I-32. The WREN-TSM is an evolution of TSM waveform used for transmissions supporting secret and below classifications. WREN-TSM provides the same functions of TSM but is security hardened with National Security Agency type 1 cryptography for government use. Porting WREN-TSMs advanced commercial waveform into the Army’s tactical radios gives Soldiers the option to run both sensitive but unclassified and secret communications on different channels, or one at a time, while maintaining complete data and voice isolation among the different security classification levels. 

###### **Warrior Robust Enhanced Network Narrowband** 

I-33. WREN-Narrowband is an Army program of record waveform on multiple vendor platforms, and answers the battlefield needs of today and tomorrow. WREN-Narrowband has wide frequency coverage as well as massive scalability. It provides mitigation against intentional and unintentional electromagnetic interference. The waveform includes secure Internet protocol network capabilities, extended network range, and interoperability between different radio platforms. WREN-Narrowband provides a highly scalable and robust waveform with simplified network management for operations in congested or contested environments. 

###### **Mobile User Objective System** 

I-34. MUOS is an advanced narrowband satellite communications replacement for the existing UHF TACSAT constellation. The MUOS waveform operates on handheld, manpack or mounted ground terminals to provide beyond line of sight or over-the-horizon voice and data communications at the tactical edge. MUOS provides a ten-fold increased capacity and more robust support by adapting a commercial 3G wideband code division multiple access cellular phone architecture, using geosynchronous satellites in place of cell towers. The MUOS waveform encapsulates all network configurations, addressing, and DODIN access protocols and is currently ported on select handheld, manpack, small form fit program for manpack radios and updated aviation radios. 

##### **SECTION II – RADIO FORM FACTORS** 

I-35. Form factors are tactical radio assets and capabilities that adhere to desired performance parameters to include specified standards for hardware and software components. Implementing industry and Army 

**31 July 2025** 

**ATP 6-02.53** 

**165** 

**Appendix I** 

standards for system size, weight and power, and cooling supports the Army’s concepts on interoperability and ensures commonality across multiple platforms and operational assets. When prototypes and fielded systems meet defined technical requirements, it reduces the need for platform-specific integration, enables fielding of evolutionary capabilities, and significantly improves reliability, reusability, and interchangeability of software modules, devices, and system components. 

#### **RADIO CONFIGURATIONS** 

I-36. The Army’s communications assets are as diverse as the requirements, operational need, and environmental conditions for the mission being supported. Tactical radio configurations play a vital role in Army operations and combat effectiveness allowing troops to coordinate movement and exchange critical information to effectively carry out mission objectives. Typical Army radios consist of a transmitter and receiver and an antenna that radiates the electromagnetic energy for signal transmission and communication exchange. Tactical radio configurations are also equipped with user controls that allow frequency, power, and channel adjustment to augment capabilities and may be mounted on various platforms allowing operations in moderate or harsh weather conditions and obscure or unconventional terrain. 

I-37. Tactical radios have evolved into highly sophisticated communications devices making them indispensable assets during all phases from pre-planning to execution of operations. With a variety of tactical radio configurations, commanders and signal Soldiers have access to a myriad of functionality and features to suit their needs. The Army inventory includes radio configurations offering voice and data transmission with advanced encryption, anti-jamming, and enhancement features such as frequency hopping for added transmission security. Configurations may include single or dual channel sidebands or both. Radio systems may also operate in varying frequency bands or ranges including HF, VHF, or UHF enabling Soldiers to stay connected with each other and their commanders at all times. 

I-38. Having such a diverse suite of radios allows Soldiers and units flexibility for long- or short-range communication while multiple host platforms—including ground (portable), airborne, maritime, and transportable—enable synchronization of military operations between dismounted troops and aircraft, land vehicles, warships or submarines. Radio configuration types include— 

- Portable. 

   - Handheld and small form fit. 

   - Manpack. 

   - Ground. 

- Vehicular. 

- Airborne. 

- Transportable. 

- Narrowband (single-channel) tactical satellite. 

##### **PORTABLE CONFIGURATIONS** 

I-39. Portable radio communications configurations are typically single, lightweight radio systems designed for the dismounted short-range communications. They consist of handheld and man-portable radios used by individual users for point-to-point communities and information sharing. These configurations typically include a battery box for rechargeable or  non-rechargeable batteries, short and medium range antennas, and other related accessories, including handsets, headphones, and carrying equipment, for operating in a dismounted mode. Some portable radio communications configurations may support vehicle configurations using specialized mounting adapters and supplementary equipment. Portable radio communications configurations include systems such as— 

- PRC-117F (manpack multiband). 

- PRC-117G (manpack multiband). 

- PRC-119 (manpack, legacy single-channel SINCGARS). 

- PRC-148 (handheld, original MBITR single-channel multiband). 

- PRC 150 (manpack, single-channel high frequency). 

- PRC-152 (multiband, handheld). 

- PRC-154 (handheld Rifleman; operates Soldier Radio Waveform. 

- PRC-158 (two-channel, multiband manpack). 

**166** 

**ATP 6-02.53** 

**31 July 2025** 

**Tactical Radio Platforms** 

- PRC 160 (improved manpack, single-channel high frequency). 

- PRC-162 (two-channel, multiband). 

- PRC-163 (two-channel, handheld Leader Radio). 

I-40. Handheld, manpack, and small form fit radios provide tactical radio communications for on-the-move, at-the-halt, and stationary line of sight and beyond line of sight communications. These handheld devices offer reduced footprint and include additional waveforms supporting simultaneous voice, data, and video communications. 

##### **HANDHELD AND SMALL FORM FIT RADIOS** 

I-41. Handheld and small form fit radios include systems that are easily carried or worn by Soldiers with personal tactical equipment or Nett Warrior ensembles normally used in dismounted operations. While some legacy handheld radios are single-channel systems, others provide two simultaneous operating channels supporting voice and data and enable line of sight and beyond line of sight communications. These radios are compatible with most legacy radios and provide joint interoperability. Two-channel handheld radios such as the Leader Radio enables net-centric operations and provide multiband and multimode functionality to deliver reliable and secure tactical communications down to the squad level. The Leader Radio can support secure voice and data using various waveforms including SINCGARS, VHF and UHF line of sight, and narrowband (single-channel) TACSAT. The Leader Radio also operates TSM and WREN advanced networking waveforms that provide connectivity to the Nett Warrior end-user device. Examples include the AN/PRC-148C/D/F and AN/PRC-163 radios. 

##### **MANPACK RADIOS** 

I-42. Manpack radios provide single-channel and two-channel secure voice and data via multiple waveforms and consist of both legacy systems and software-defined radios such as the AN/PRC-158 and the AN/PRC162. These radios enable communication exchange in austere environments between units and higher headquarters without having to rely on a fixed infrastructure. 

###### **AN/PRC-104** 

I-43. The AN/PRC-104 is a legacy HF radio that operates between 3 and 30 MHz for long-range communication. It operates in both upper sideband and lower sideband modes for voice communications; supports continuous wave for Morse code; and enables frequency shift keying for transmission of teletype signals or other data. The AN/PRC-104 can be configured for vehicular or fixed-station use with proper accessories. The basic manpack configuration is comprised of— 

- AM-6874/AN/PRC-104 (20-watt RF amplifier/coupler). 

- CY-7541 (battery case and battery pack). 

- RT-1209/URC (power receiver transmitter). 

- AN-PRC 217A (whip antenna). 

- Handset H-189/GR and telegraph key (KY-872/PRC-104). 

**_Note._** The legacy AN/PRC-104 is replaced by AN/PRC-150 and-160 with ITN modernization upgrades. 

###### **AN/PRC-117F and AN/PRC-117G, Falcon III Family of Multiband Radios** 

I-44. Both the AN/PRC-117F and AN/PRC-117G radios are single-channel software-defined manpack systems supporting voice and data for mounted and dismounted operations. They are designed to provide reliable tactical communications in a small, lightweight package that can maximize user mobility. The AN/PRC-117F systems support multiple waveforms including— 

- SINCGARS and VHF line of sight. 

- Single-channel UHF (30 MHz–512) narrowband (single-channel) TACSAT.  DAMA. 

   - Integrated waveform. 

- Havequick II (supports ground-to-air voice communications). 

**31 July 2025** 

**ATP 6-02.53** 

**167** 

**Appendix I** 

I-45. The AN/PRC-117G supports new capabilities offering significant technological upgrades to the AN/PRC-117F. These capabilities provide advantages including— 

- SINCGARS and VHF line of sight. 

- Single-channel UHF line of sight and SATCOM. 

   - DAMA. 

   - Integrated waveform. 

- MUOS-ready hardware. 

- Harris Advanced Networking Waveform (GEN I and II). 

- L-band network (up to 2 GHz). 

**_Note._** The AN/PSC-5 C/D, AN/PRC-117F, and AN/PRC-117G radios do not meet cryptographic modernization requirements as mandated by DOD and the National Security Agency and will be replaced by the AN/PRC-158 or AN/PRC-162 handheld, manpack and small form fit radios. 

I-46. The AN/PRC-117G radio is capable of simultaneously transmitting Voice over Internet Protocol and digital data such as file transfers, chat, streaming video, and position location information on a single channel. The AN/PRC-117G also supports SINCGARS networks and SATURN .which replaces Havequick II for airground voice communications. 

**_Note._** The AN/PRC-117F and AN/PRC-117G radios were limited purchases of commercial offthe-shelf) multiband multi-mission radios requisitioned specifically to fill vacancies for narrowband (single-channel) TACSAT radios, normally requiring AN/PSC-5 UHF SATCOM authorizations. 

###### **AN/PRC-119 and RT-1523 Series** 

I-47. The AN/PRC-119 is included in the SINCGARS family of radios that uses the RT-1523. The AN/PRC119 is designed to ensure commonality among various ground (vehicular), airborne, and manpack configurations. The individual components of SINCGARS radios are interchangeable from one configuration to the next, a design feature that reduces the burden on the logistics system for repair parts. It has evolved from a hardware-driven module to the ASIP radio configuration that includes a software base to complement smaller and lighter design requirements and the ability to reprogram the SINCGARS to further improve the radio overtime. 

**_Note._** The RT-1523 radio series includes a diverse set of systems including the RT-1523/A/B and E/F and the AN/PRC-119 radios. It will continue to support the SINCGARS waveform and its associate future upgrades. The combat net radio will replace the AN/PRC-119 (RT-1523) series legacy radios through attrition. The RT-1523 C/D radio models are no longer fielded to Army units. 

###### **AN/PRC-148, Leader Radio Series** 

I-48. The AN/PRC-148 MBITR and handheld, manpack small form fit Leader Radio series are handheld, Soldier-worn two channel radios that provide interoperable communications to Soldiers needing an on-themove or at-the-halt capability, whether mounted or dismounted. 

I-49. The legacy AN/PRC-148 MBITR is used for special operations forces and company size networks depending on command guidance and mission requirements. When used as a handheld radio, the AN/PRC148 MBITR supports the secure requirements for a platoon, squad, or team. The AN/PRC-148 MBITR enables small-unit leaders’ adequate control of subordinate elements activities and can perform ground-toair, ship-to-shore, DAMA TACSAT, civil, military, and multinational communications. The AN/PRC-148 MBITR consists of a portable, battery-operated transceiver capable of operating in non-secure analog or secure digital voice and data. The AN/PRC-148 MBITR is software upgradeable to add the following capabilities— 

- SINCGARS (including Retransmission). 

**168** 

**ATP 6-02.53** 

**31 July 2025** 

**Tactical Radio Platforms** 

- Narrowband (single channel) TACSAT. 

   - DAMA. 

   - Integrated waveform. 

- Advanced narrowband digital voice terminal/KYV-5. 

- Havequick I/II. 

I-50. Planners and operators can configure the AN/PRC-148 MBITR manually using the radio control panel or through a software-based application. The software-based application has an interface that allows uploading and downloading assigned frequency lists, waveform types, and radio power settings. Once configured, the AN/PRC-148 MBITR can distribute configuration information or clone to another AN/PRC148 MBITR. This cloning feature allows the G-6 or S-6 spectrum manager to disseminate technical information down the tactical echelons to each radio. The AN/PRC-148 MBITR system is suited for urban operations where communication signals are challenged by terrain or man-made structures. For these conditions, the AN/PRC-148 MBITR system provides a retransmission capability called back-to-back. The retransmission configuration requires two AN/PRC-148 MBITRs and a small cable kit with electronic filters. The radios repeat the transmission with no loss of signal quality. 

I-51. The AN/PRC‐148D/F series radios are versions of the handheld, manpack, and small form fit twochannel Leaders Radio and are enhanced versions of the legacy AN/PRC‐148 MBITR radio. Its functionality is comparable to the existing MBITR radio operating SINCGARS and TACSAT integrated waveform on one channel, but includes a second channel capable of operating TSM and WREN narrowband networking waveforms. 

I-52. Handheld, manpack and small form fit Leader Radios provide voice communications including support for data services such as text, control graphics, and imagery through interoperability with tactical end-user devices or Nett Warrior systems. Leader Radio systems include the full rate production two-channel AN/PRC-148/D/F series, AN/PRC-163(v)2, and AN/PRC-163(v)3 systems. These systems operate SINCGARS and networking waveforms such as the TSM, WREN, single-channel TACSAT, and SATURN. Some configurations support mounting the leader radio to extend its communication range which provides additional amplification on the narrowband SINCGARS channel and TSM wideband channel. 

###### **AN/PRC-150** 

I-53. The AN/PRC-150 HF radio provides tactical forces, homeland defense forces, and emergency operations elements with secure, terrain-independent line of sight and beyond line of sight voice and data communications. The AN/PRC-150 radio does not rely on retransmission or SATCOM. The radio provides long distance, wide area, gap free, fixed or on the move, ground, ground to air communications via plain text, secure analog voice with robust data, and digital voice modes and advanced serial tone electromagnetic counter-countermeasures modem Internet Protocol networking. 

I-54. The AN/PRC-150 supports red and black key management and ALE link protection. The AN/PRC150 vehicular radio systems provide units with beyond line of sight communications without having to rely on satellite availability on a saturated tactical communications network. The systems’ manpack and vehicular configurations have reliable communications while on the move and allow for rapid transmission of data and imagery to display the common operational picture. The legacy AN/PRC-150 is replaced by AN/PRC-160 with ITN modernization upgrades. 

I-55. When employed, the AN/PRC-150 radio supports single-channel radio operations, and frequency hopping radio operations. The AN/PRC-150 has the following characteristics and capabilities— 

- Operates from 1.6–29.9999 MHz using skywave modulation with selectable low, medium, and high output power. It also operates from 20.0000–59.9999 MHz frequency modulation with a maximum output of 10.0 watts. 

- Uses embedded type 1 multinational cryptographic algorithms for secure voice and data communications between ground and aircraft. 

- Interfaces with SINCGARS cryptographic ignition key embedded in the removable keypad. 

- Uses advanced electromagnetic counter-countermeasures serial-tone frequency hopping to improve communications reliability in jamming environments. 

- Supports frequency hopping in HF narrowband, wideband, and list. 

**31 July 2025** 

**ATP 6-02.53** 

**169** 

**Appendix I** 

- Employs programmable system presets for one-button operation. 

- Provides internal tuning unit that matches a wide variety of whip, dipole, and long-wire antenna automatically. 

- Uses internal, high-speed serial-tone modem that provides data operation up to 9,600 bits per second. 

- Uses embedded ALE digital voice 600 simplifies HF operation by automatically selecting an accepted channel. 

- Supports North Atlantic Treaty Organization STANAG 4538 automatic radio control system link setup and data link protocols in third-generation ALE radio mode. 

- Supports point-to-point protocol or ethernet networking capabilities. 

- Supports North Atlantic Treaty Organization STANAG 4538-compliant wireless Internet Protocol data transfer. 

- Supports frequency hopping in HF narrowband, wideband, and list. 

###### **AN/PRC-152A** 

I-56. The AN/PRC-152A radio is a wideband networking handheld radio that provides simultaneous voice, video, and high-speed data in a portable form factor. The AN/PRC-152A radio delivers ad hoc, self-healing, and adaptive networking capabilities to dismounted Soldiers on the move. The voice and data capabilities of the AN/PRC-152A are National Security Agency-certified to encrypt networks classified up to top secret. The AN/PRC-152 includes an embedded GPS receiver to display local position and to provide automatic position reporting for situational awareness. The vehicular version of the AN/PRC-152 is the AN/VRC-110. 

**_Note._** The AN/PRC-152A does not meet cryptographic modernization requirements as mandated by the DOD and the National Security Agency and is being replaced by the AN/PRC-148C/D or AN/PRC-163 Leader Radios. 

###### **AN/PRC-158, GEN II Handheld, Manpack and Small Form Fit Manpack** 

I-57. The upgraded AN/PRC‐158 is a two‐channel software‐defined radio that hosts several waveforms and provides secure Internet Protocol-based voice and data communication. The radio set configurations provide networking transport to extend communication from brigade and battalion to company and platoon radios. The upgraded AN/PRC-158 replaces the legacy AN/PRC-158, Falcon IV as well as the handheld, manpack, and small form fit, GEN I, AN/PRC-155. 

I-58. The legacy AN/PRC-158 radio is a multichannel manpack radio equipped with dual channel connectivity to provide forward-deployed Soldiers with simultaneous send and receive capabilities in a compact and lightweight form factor. The AN/PRC-158 routing and crossbanding technologies support communications redundancy, voice, and data communications. The AN/PRC-158 is National Security Agency-certified radio that is secure up to top secret security classification. The AN/PRC-158 covers the multiband frequency range, with up to 10 watts of transmit power for narrowband waveforms and up to 20 watts for SATCOM and wideband waveforms. The AN/PRC-158 supports the following waveforms: 

- SINCGARS. 

- VHF and UHF line of sight. 

- SATURN. 

- MUOS. 

- UHF SATCOM. 

   - Integrated waveform. 

   - DAMA. 

- TSM. 

- WREN. 

###### **AN/PRC-160(V)** 

I-59. The AN/PRC‐160(V) expands secure HF technology by adding advanced encryption capabilities. This radio system provides enhanced wideband data performance and interoperability with legacy radios, 

**170** 

**ATP 6-02.53** 

**31 July 2025** 

**Tactical Radio Platforms** 

AN/PRC-104, AN/PRC-150, and AN/PRC-160. The AN/PRC‐160(V) is a single‐channel, half‐duplex, tactical, HF transceiver that provides secure voice and data transmission. The AN/PRC-160(V) radio uses a single battery, which reduces the overall weight and provides 20-watt HF and 10-watts VHF transmission with continuous coverage from 1.5–59.9999 MHz. The AN/PRC‐160(V) transmits data at frequencies from 3 kHz–24 kHz, at data rates exceeding 120 kilobits per second. The AN/PRC‐160(V) uses software programmable encryption for both legacy and modern voice and data. The AN/PRC-160(V) replaces legacy AN /PRC 160 manpack radio which provides accurate position location and enhanced situational awareness using embedded selective availability anti-spoofing module and GPS receivers and secure voice and data confidentiality protection up to top secret classification level. 

###### **AN/PRC-162, Gen II Handheld, Manpack, and Small Form Fit Manpack** 

I-60. The AN/PRC‐162 is a two channel manpack radio that provides line of sight and beyond line of sight, high-bandwidth waveforms for on‐the‐move voice and data communications for mounted or dismounted Soldiers. The AN/PRC-162 can be mounted in a vehicle configuration that provides the capability for realtime sharing of data, images, voice, and video between assets on the battlefield. It supports retransmission service and can crossband voice or data between two separate networks. The AN/PRC-162 provides highspeed mobile ad hoc and networked communications, point-to-point data and voice networking, nextgeneration satellite, and MUOS communications. The AN/PRC-162 offers interoperability with legacy waveforms and has an open-architecture design to facilitate upgrades. The AN/PRC is interoperable with the following: 

- SINCGARS. 

- VHF and UHF line of sight. 

- SATURN. 

- MUOS. 

- UHF SATCOM. 

   - Integrated waveform. 

   - DAMA. 

- TSM. 

- WREN. 

I-61. The legacy AN/PRC-162 radio provides high-speed mobile ad hoc networked communications, pointto-point data and voice next-generation SATCOM, and MUOS and can be configured for vehicular and airborne platforms. The AN/PRC-162 radio provides the capability for real-time sharing of data, images, voice, and video between assets on the battlefield; offers interoperability with legacy waveforms and has an open-architecture design to facilitate upgrades. Handheld, manpack, vehicular, and airborne versions are available to support army tactical radio operations. 

###### **AN/PRC-163(V)2, Leader Radio** 

I-62. The AN/PRC‐163(V)2 provides crossbanding, interoperability, and redundancy in a small form factor. The AN/PRC-163(V)2 provides Soldiers with status information at a glance, which allows them to access basic functions without removing the radio from its holster. The radio provides two discrete radio modules, each capable of multi‐band, multi-mode operation. The AN/PRC‐163(V)2 replaces legacy AN/PRC‐163 handheld radio which allows Soldiers to receive mission critical information by viewing a liquid crystal display screen. The intelligence, surveillance, and reconnaissance video module provides full-motion video capabilities for advanced situational awareness. The AN/PRC-163 is interoperable with— 

- TSM waveform. 

- SATURN. 

- SINCGARS system improvement program and ASIP. 

- Integrated waveform. 

- SATURN. 

**31 July 2025** 

**ATP 6-02.53** 

**171** 

**Appendix I** 

###### **AN/PRC-166** 

I-63. The AN/PRC‐166 provides real‐time, continuous position location information. The AN/PRC‐166 is designed to track personnel and assets and provide situational awareness to prevent fratricide. It easily connects to tablets or laptops and is mountable onto robotic or unmanned aerial vehicles. 

###### **AN/PRC-168(V)1** 

I-64. The AN/PRC‐168(V)1 includes an expanded frequency range, enhanced network throughput to stream high-definition videos. It is interoperable with compatible devices that offer TSM waveform functionality for tactical operations. 

###### **AN/PRC-170** 

I-65. The AN/PRC-170 is a small form factor TSM radio system. It includes enhancements which provide Soldiers with interoperable voice, streaming video, and data communications. The radio supports long wavelength band, satellite band frequencies (1250–2600 MHz) and UHF (225–450 MHz) communications. 

###### **AN/PRC-171** 

I-66. The AN/PRC-171 is a small form factor, wideband mobile ad hoc network radio that operates in the UHF and long wavelength band frequency range (225–2600 MHz) with a power range of up to 3.2 watts. The AN/PRC-171 radio is compatible with the AN/PRC-163(V)2 handheld Leader Radio batteries and accessories. 

###### **AN/PRC-2064** 

I-67. The AN/PRC‐2064 is an interoperability system to interface otherwise incompatible systems compatible. A tactical voice bridge enables interoperability between different radio networks operating over any frequency range or waveform. 

##### **GENERAL GROUND CONFIGURATIONS** 

I-68. The ground radio communications configurations are single or multiple radio system configurations that provide high power, long-range communications for permanent or semi-permanent fixed sites, operating bases, or installations to include airfield or forward operations base. Ground radio communications configurations are not typically designed for transit or transportation and rely on permanent installation hardware, commercial power, and larger antennas. Ground radio communications configurations include the AN/GRC-100 series single-channel VHF radio used for ground to air communications. 

##### **VEHICULAR CONFIGURATIONS** 

I-69. The vehicular radio communications configurations are characterized by their rugged and enduring performance capabilities. Available for single-or dual-radio mounting, the vehicular-mounted tactical radios provide interoperability and high performance to units in a variety of operational environments, including remote environments characterized by harsh conditions. Configurations are customizable for a variety of missions requirements and include mobile assets, both ground and airborne. The Army inventory of vehicular-mounted assets includes the AN/PRC-117 (L3 Harris family), AN/PRC-148 C/D/F (manpack and mounted Leader Radios), AN/PRC-150, AN/PRC-158, AN/PRC-162, AN/PRC-163, AN/VRC-100, and the RT-1523 series. 

###### **AN/VRC-100** 

I-70. The AN/VRC-100 is an improved legacy HF, AM ground and vehicular radio that provides multifunctional, full digital signal processing. It is used for a variety of ground or mobile applications. Its primary role in the Army is for supporting tactical air traffic control and operations centers needing beyond line of sight ground to air communications with aircraft equipped with HF systems such as the AN/ARC-220 radio. The AN/VRC-100 radio is a fully integrated plug-and-play multi-mode voice or data communications system configured in a portable case that allows substantial distance communications beyond line of sight by 

**172** 

**ATP 6-02.53** 

**31 July 2025** 

**Tactical Radio Platforms** 

providing users the ability to maintain contact during short, mid, and long-range operations. As an advanced data communications system, the AN/VRC-100 provides reliable digital connectivity. 

I-71. The AN/VRC-100 radio uses the RT, power amplifier coupler, and control display unit line replaceable units of the AN/ARC-220 system without modification, within an aluminum-structured, bracketed case. The AN/VRC-100 has a portable, metal case, with a removable top, that provides easy access for removal of line replaceable units. All controls and the radio input and output are located on the front panel. The AN/VRC100 increases the situational awareness of aviation assets. The AN/VRC-100 has capabilities that are identical to the AN/ARC-220 HF radio, which makes the AN/VRC-100 radio an ideal radio to support AN/ARC-220 HF radio equipped airborne platforms. Key features of the AN/VRC-100 are— 

- Full digital signal processing with embedded ALE, electromagnetic protection, and data modem. 

- Spare card slot in the RT provides for future growth. 

- Operates on 28 volts direct current (and is compatible with 24 volts direct current vehicular power) or from 115 or 220 volts alternating current 50 or 60 Hertz (Hz) power source. 

- A personal computer or laptop connectivity. 

- E-mail messaging using the local RS-232 interface. 

- Capability to tune a variety of antennas. 

**_Note._** The AN/VRC-100 requires the KY-100M to provide encryption capabilities. The KY-100M is cryptographic modernized according to DOD and National Security Agency requirements. 

I-72. Table I-2 lists the three configurations of the AN/VRC-100. Refer to the operator technical manual for more information on the AN/VRC-100(V) 1/2/3.

###### **_RT-1523 SINCGARS_** 

I-73. Either the RT-1523 A/B/C/D or the RT-1523E (ground version receiver transmitter) comprise the core component of all ground-based radio sets. The RT-1523 series has internal COMSEC circuits, so they do not require additional cryptographic hardware for secure communications. The ground versions have a whisper mode for noise restriction during patrols or while in defensive positions. The radio operator whispers into the handset while being heard at the receiver in a normal voice. There are six ground unit versions of the SINCGARS (RT-1523/A/B and E/F). Table I-3 on page 174 compares the components of several versions of SINCGARS radios.

##### **AIRBORNE CONFIGURATIONS** 

I-74. Airborne radio configurations play a vital role in providing communications between ground and airborne elements and communications between individual aircraft and formations. These radios are critical enablers essential to close air support, search and rescue, resupply, medical and casualty evacuation mission objectives. Airborne radio configurations are used for aviation platforms and provide line of sight and beyond line of sight communications supporting all aviation operations. line of sight communications systems supporting air-to-air and air-to-ground aviation operations. These single system configurations include adapters, installation hardware, and integration components to enable crew operations while on the aircraft, during flight, or while stationary on the ground. The airborne radio configurations supports traditional cockpit controls or human machine interface settings. The airborne radio configurations include such systems as— 

- ARC 201, 201D (SC legacy SINCGARS radio). 

- ARC 210 (SC multiband radio). 

- ARC 220 (SC HF radio). 

- ARC 231 (SC multiband, software-defined radio). 

###### **AN/ARC-201** 

I-75. The AN/ARC-201 SINCGARS radio is a tactical airborne radio subsystem that provides secure, antijam voice and data communications. Ground and airborne versions are interoperable even though they each have distinct physical characteristics. The significant change in the airborne mode is the faceplate attached to the various configurations and the add-on modules that change each version’s capabilities. Airborne versions RT-1476, RT-1477A/B/C, and RT-1478 require the KY-58 security equipment for ciphertext operations. 

**_Note._** A new cryptographic compliant air ground network radio will replace the AN/ARC-201 radio. The air ground network will continue to support the SINCGARS waveform and provide MUOS and TSM to airborne rotary wing platforms. 

###### **_RT-1476_** 

I-76. The RT-1476, AN/ARC-201, is the base radio in all three versions; each version operates in the singlechannel and frequency hopping modes. The RT-1476, AN/ARC-201 typically mounts in the cockpit of an aircraft. 

###### **_RT-1477_** 

I-77. The RT-1477 mounts in an isolated equipment compartment on the aircraft and consists of radio and a radio set control, C-11466. The radio set control is co-located with the pilot and can remotely control from the cockpit. Dedicated cables transmit control and status signals back and forth between the RT-1477 and radio set control. The RT-1477 is capable of retransmission operation. 

**174** 

**ATP 6-02.53** 

**31 July 2025** 

**Tactical Radio Platforms** 

###### **_RT-1478_** 

I-78. The RT-1478 mounts in the remote equipment compartment of the aircraft and operates in conjunction with the aircraft’s system control display unit. The optional data rate adapter enables the radio to process 1,200 and 2,400 Hz frequency. The operation of the data rate adapter is automatic, and no operator interface is necessary. 

###### **AN/ARC-210** 

I-79. The AN/ARC-210 offered in several models, which when coupled with ancillary equipment, provides the aviation community with exceptional long-range capability. The RT-1556B provides line of sight VHF and UHF capability, SINCGARS, MUOS, and SATURN electromagnetic counter-countermeasures waveforms. The RT-1794I, RT-1824I, RT-1851I, and RT-1851AI are network capable and include embedded cryptographic algorithms, 5 kHz and 25 kHz and DAMA SATCOM. 

I-80. The AN/ARC-210 radio provides air-to-air and air-to-ground, two-way voice communications via UHF and VHF. The embedded SATCOM functions that operate in the UHF radio band data and voice communications. The AN/ARC-210 is integrated on Army fixed wing platforms only. It provides the following key features— 

- 30–400 MHz frequency range provides VHF and UHF in all radios; 121.5 and 243.0 MHz guard channels, and four-channel scan. 

- 30–512 MHz frequency range providing VHF and UHF in the RT-1851AI; 121.5 and 243.0 MHz guard channels, four-channel scan. 

- Tuning capability: 5 kHz with remote control, 2.5 kHz via 1553 bus. 

- Optional power amplifiers, mounts, and low noise amplifier and diplexer. 

**_Note._** A new cryptographic compliant air ground network radio will replace the AN/ARC-210 radio. The air ground network will continue to support the SINCGARS waveform and provide MUOS and TSM to airborne rotary wing platforms. 

###### **AN/ARC-220** 

I-81. The AN/ARC-220 radio system is a microprocessor-based HF communications system intended for airborne applications. The ground version of the AN/ARC-220 is the AN/VRC-100. The AN/ARC-220 radio system uses advanced digital signal processor technology to provide two-way communication. 

I-82. The AN/ARC-220 consists of three interchangeable units; an RT (RT-1749/URC or RT-1749A/URC), power amplifier coupler (AM-7531/URC), and control display unit (C-12436/URC). The AN/ARC-220 has embedded ALE, serial tone data modem, and anti-jamming functions. The AN/ARC-220 provides the electrical interface with other AN/ARC-220 and associated aircraft systems. The AN/ARC-220 can program up to twenty-five free text data messages in real time and retrieve data messages to be stored for later viewing. The AN/ARC-220 radio system provides the following capabilities— 

- Frequency range: 2–29.9999 MHz in 100 Hz steps. 

- Twenty user programmable simplex or half-duplex channels. 

- Twelve programmable electromagnetic countermeasures hop sets. 

- Certified for ALE. 

- An integrated data modem that enables communication in noisy environments. 

- Built-in integration with external GPS units allows position data report submission with the push of a button. 

- Embedded ALE, electromagnetic countermeasures, and data. 

- Rapid and efficient tuning to a variety of antennas. 

**_Note._** The AN/ARC-220 requires the KY-100M to provide encryption capabilities. The KY-100M is cryptographic modernized according to DOD and National Security Agency requirements.

**Appendix I** 

###### **AN/ARC-231** 

I-83. The AN/ARC-231 is a multiband multi-mission airborne VHF and UHF radio capable line of sight and SATCOM radio system that has multiband multi-mission, secure anti-jam voice and data capabilities. The RT-1808 is the primary RT for the AN/ARC-231 that capitalizes on the AN/PSC-5 Spitfire family’s expandable architecture and permits users to upgrade as new requirements drive new capabilities. The AN/ARC-231, used in the Army Airborne Command and Control System, provides communications capabilities to corps, division maneuver brigades, commander’s airborne tactical command posts, or individual attack and lift helicopters. AN/ARC-231 radios will implement the SATURN waveform to replace Havequick I and II capabilities. The AN/ARC-231 has the following characteristics and capabilities— 

- SINCGARS communications modes. 

- DAMA and integrated waveform tactical SATCOM modes. 

- Frequency ranges of— 

   - 30–87.975 MHz VHF and SINCGARS. 

   - 108–173.995 MHz VHF AM and FM modes. 

   - 225–399.995 MHz UHF AM and UHF SATCOM band. 

   - 403–511.995 MHz UHF modulation, public service band. 

- Embedded COMSEC and transmission security keys with transmitting and receive over-the-air rekeying. 

- 148 channel presets. 

- Embedded data control waveform analog to digital converter and tactical Internet Protocol. 

- Access to 5 kHz and 25 kHz UHF SATCOM channels. 

- 8.33 kHz air traffic control channelization coverage to 512 Hz. 

- Minimal size and weight are suitable for installation in rotary wing and fixed-wing platforms. 

**_Note._** The AN/ARC-231 (RT-1808) radio will be replaced by cryptographic compliant AN/ARC231A (RT-1987) radio. 

##### **TRANSPORTABLE (GROUND) CONFIGURATIONS** 

I-84. The transportable (ground) radio communications configurations include single or multiple radio systems designed to support communications for command posts operations or fixed locations and are not capable of operation while in transit. Configurations are transportable modular systems housed in selfcontained transit cases and include lightweight adapters or docking stations, installation hardware, and power supplies designed for movement, when necessary, to support various tactical requirements. Transportable radio communications configurations include the AN/TRC 214 series. 

##### **NARROWBAND (SINGLE-CHANNEL) TACTICAL SATELLITE** 

I-85. Narrowband (single-channel) TACSAT radios are small, lightweight, manpack, multiband, multimode VHF and UHF radios. Narrowband (single-channel) TACSAT radios also provide secure voice communications and situational awareness for special operations forces, airborne, air assault, infantry divisions, and infantry brigades. 

I-86. The Army uses narrowband (single-channel) TACSAT radio and other satellite capabilities to provide long haul, worldwide communications coverage to support critical command and control communications to ground and mobile operating forces. 

I-87. Narrowband (single-channel) TACSAT radios provide range extension for voice and data. The beyond line of sight range extension capability is used in the Army’s SATCOM on-the-move OE-563 functionality in moving vehicular platforms, versus stationary. Narrowband terminals are preferred for initial communications in contingency situations because of mobility and size advantages. Their disadvantages include difficulty in obtaining access to the UHF range of the EMS and the lack of anti-jam capability for threat mitigation. 

I-88. Narrowband (single-channel) TACSAT radios offer interoperability with integrated waveform terminals and DAMA when accessing non-processed narrowband SATCOM and offer greater satellite access and automated channel sharing, though at reduced data rates. This limits normal access to 2400 bits per

second with advanced narrowband digital voice terminal provided voice and data; however, with voice encoder and mixed excitation linear prediction upgrades, voice quality and clarity are vastly improved. Integrated waveform provides access improvements of up to four times the rate seen in DAMA on a 25 kHz channel. Capabilities associated with integrated waveform include— 

- Network communications. 

- Preplanned support to operations. 

- Ad hoc communications. 

- Point-to-point calls 

###### **Shadowfire, AN/PSC-5C** 

I-89. The Shadowfire is a field upgrade of the AN/PSC-5 Spitfire terminal. The upgrade provides all the capabilities of the AN/PSC-5 and additional capabilities for SATURN and SINCGARS anti-jam. The ability to receive and transmit over-the-air rekeying or over-the-air transfer, extended 30–420 MHz frequency range, access to 5 kHz and 25 kHz UHF SATCOM channels, and embedded data control waveform controller. 

I-90. Additional features include range extension and mixed excitation linear prediction voice coding, 142 preset channels, advanced key loading, DS-101 fill capability and embedded tactical Internet Protocol and the cryptographic algorithm. The Shadowfire operates in the VHF and UHF spectrum and supports line of sight with frequency agile modes, SATCOM, DAMA, and maritime operation. Voice and data operations are available in each of these modes. The Shadowfire also provides enhanced mixed excitation linear prediction, vocoder and improved linear predictive coding, anti-jam communications, and over-the-air rekeying and over-the-air transfer capabilities. 

**_Note._** DS-101 is an interface standard for electronic transfer devices or fill units developed and endorsed by the US National Security Agency. 

###### **AN/PSC-5D** 

I-91. The multiband multi-mission radio, AN/PSC-5D, offers a higher frequency range than the Spitfire and Shadowfire. The AN/PSC-5D provides lightweight, secure, network-capable, multiband multi-mission, antijam, voice, imagery, and data communications capability in a single package. The AN/PSC-5D operates in the VHF and UHF spectrum band and supports line of sight with frequency agile modes, SATCOM, DAMA, and maritime operation. Voice and data operations are available in each of these modes. Table I-4 on page 178 outlines a line of sight interoperability comparison of the AN/PSC-5 family of radios and the AN/PRC117F. For more information on narrowband (single-channel) TACSAT and DAMA, refer to the equipment operator manual.

**ATP 6-02.53** 

**31 July 2025** 

**Glossary** 

##### **SECTION II – TERMS** 

###### **adversary** 

A party acknowledged as potentially hostile to a friendly party and against which the use of force may be envisaged. (JP 3-0) 

###### **communications security** 

Protective measures to deny unauthorized access to information of value derived from use of communications systems. (JP 6-0) 

###### **Department of Defense information network** 

The information capabilities and associated processes owned or leased by the Department of Defense for collecting, processing, storing, disseminating, and managing information, whether interconnected or stand-alone. (JP 6-0) 

###### **Department of Defense information network-Army** 

An Army-operated enclave of the Department of Defense information network that encompasses all Army information capabilities that collect, process, store, display, disseminate, and protect information worldwide. (ATP 6-02.71) 

###### **electromagnetic interference** 

Any electromagnetic disturbance, induced intentionally or unintentionally, that interrupts, obstructs, or otherwise degrades or limits the effective performance of electromagnetic spectrum-dependent systems and electrical equipment. (JP 3-85) 

###### **electromagnetic operational environment** 

A composite of the actual and potential electromagnetic energy radiation, conditions, circumstances, and influences that affect the employment of capabilities and the decisions of the commander. (JP 3- 85) 

###### **electromagnetic order of battle** 

A subset of the overall order of battle that consists of the identification, strength, command structure, disposition, and operating parameters of the electromagnetic spectrum-dependent systems. (JP 3-85) 

###### **electromagnetic jamming** 

The deliberate radiation, reradiation, or reflection of electromagnetic energy for the purpose of preventing or reducing an enemy’s effective use of the electromagnetic spectrum, with the intent of degrading or neutralizing the enemy’s combat capability. (JP 3-85) 

###### **electromagnetic pulse** 

A strong burst of electromagnetic radiation caused by a nuclear explosion, energy weapon, or by natural phenomenon, that may couple with electrical or electronic systems to produce damaging current and voltage surges. (JP 3-85) 

###### **emission control** 

The selective and controlled use of electromagnetic, acoustic, or other emitters to optimize command and control capabilities while minimizing, for operations security: a. detection by enemy sensors, b. mutual interference among friendly systems, and/or c. enemy interference with the ability to execute a military deception plan. (JP 3-85) 

###### **enemy** 

A party identified as hostile against which the use of force is authorized. (ADP 3-0) 

###### ***net control station** 

A communications station designated to control traffic and enforce circuit discipline within a given net. 

###### **operational environment** 

The aggregate of the conditions, circumstances, and influences that affect the employment of capabilities and bear on the decisions of the commander. (JP 3-0) 

**31 July 2025** 

**ATP 6-02.53** 

**183** 

**Glossary** 

###### **threat** 

Any combination of actors, entities, or forces that have the capability and intent to harm United States forces, United States national interests, or the homeland. (ADP 3-0)
